## T-330/T-331 — Workstream D: ledger-driven MC forward projection + bootstrap CI (✅ MERGED TO MAIN, 2026-07-11, `81cfb20`)

**Both tasks complete.** `scripts/mc_forward_projection.py` gained `--from-ledger {T1,T2,T3,T4M-PROJ}` (T-330/D1): `derive_ledger_stats()` reads win_rate/win_mult/loss_mult/stake_frac/cadence directly from `filter_clean()`-ed live ledger records (reusing `run_band_monitor.py`'s loaders and clean_n filter verbatim, `run_power_calc.py`'s cadence derivation) instead of the module's hardcoded constants, and emits three explicit scenarios per band: **floor** (zero-edge breakeven win_rate — "what if the edge is illusory"), **operational** (the frozen `SimParams()` defaults — the original GG-071/ADR-0040 regression anchor, untouched), **ceiling** (fully observed win_rate/mult/stake from the ledger). `--bootstrap` (T-331/D2) resamples clean records with replacement (`n_boot=30, boot_sims=500` defaults — tuned down from an initial 200/1000 after empirical timing showed `simulate_paths()` cost is dominated by `days*bets_per_day` loop iterations, not `n_sims`; high-cadence T4M-PROJ costs ~1s/resample regardless) and reports P(kill) as a median + 90% CI band per horizon, e.g. "P(kill 1yr) = X% [90% CI: lo%–hi%]", directly on `build_ledger_report()`'s floor/ceiling scenarios (operational is unaffected by ledger sampling by design, so no CI is computed for it).

**Live smoke test results (not asserted in tests, informational only):** T1 (clean_n=65) floor shows 69.6%/100%/100% kill risk (30d/182d/365d) vs. ceiling (real edge) 2.8%/17.7%/17.8% — stark contrast confirms the scenario framework correctly isolates edge-vs-mechanics. T4M-PROJ (clean_n=271) ceiling shows ~0% kill risk at all horizons (win_rate=0.6568 relative to very low stake_frac≈0.057%); floor still shows material 1yr risk (~7.25%). T1's ceiling bootstrap CI is very wide (1yr: 0%–100% at n_boot=30) — expected, honest reflection of small-sample (clean_n=65) uncertainty, not a bug. T2 (clean_n=0) correctly raises a clean `ValueError`/`SystemExit(1)` rather than crashing.

**Tests:** `tests/test_mc_forward_projection.py` grew from 11 to 33 tests (all new classes: `TestDeriveLedgerStats`, `TestBuildLedgerScenarios`, `TestLoadCleanBandRecords`, `TestBootstrapKillCI`, `TestBuildLedgerReport`), synthetic/hermetic (`_mk_recs()` helper, no live-file dependency), all passing. Full repo suite run: 2464 passed, 29 skipped, **1 pre-existing failure** (`test_band_monitor.py::TestT1RegressionAnchor` — already logged as GG-106, live-ledger-drift pinning issue, unrelated to this workstream, not touched by this branch). Exit criteria for both D1 ("one command reproduces the A/B/C/E-style table from live data, no hand-entered constants") and D2 ("MC output reads as a CI band, not a single number") are met.

## T-329 — GG-105 DS-review diagnostic (1) of 3: size-weighted tercile re-run — DONE (2026-07-10)

**Did:** Selected and ran diagnostic (1) of the three GG-105 proposed diagnostics (`T324_T1_VS_T4_DIVERGENCE_MEMO.md`) first: size-weighted re-run of T4M-PROJ's clean_n edge-tercile inversion, testing whether Kelly sizing compounds the win-rate inversion's P&L damage. Built `scripts/run_t329_size_weighted_diag.py` (reuses `run_band_monitor.py`'s `load_t4_ledger`/`filter_clean` and `ab_harness.metrics()` verbatim). **Result: sizing does NOT amplify the inversion — it's inverted.** Avg stake falls across edge terciles (does not rise), and $-weighted return (`pnl_per_dollar_staked`) rises across terciles (does not fall) — tercile 3 (lowest win rate, 58.2%) has the best $/staked return of the three. Overall clean_n cohort: +8.12% pnl/staked. Candidate (b) (sizing-as-amplifier) is ruled out as GG-105 characterized it. Full detail: DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-10 (T-329 row), GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md GG-105.

## T-332 — GG-105 DS-review diagnostic (2) of 3: game-state segmentation — DONE (2026-07-11)

**Did:** Registered T-332 (operator: "register and then proceed with addressing with full depth") and ran diagnostic (2): game-state segmentation of T4M-PROJ's clean_n edge-tercile inversion, testing candidate (a) (real signal / game-state noise — T4's core latency thesis). Discovered and empirically validated (271/271, 100%, 0 collisions) a join from resolved position → live-poll cycle via `forecast_id`'s embedded `cycle_id`, against `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl`'s `(cycle_id, ticker)` key — two other candidate join sources (`paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` closed positions, `audit_mlb_t4_edge_decisions.jsonl`) were checked and ruled out first, neither carries game-state fields. Built `scripts/run_t332_gamestate_diag.py` (reuses `run_band_monitor.py` loaders + the audited `run_exp017_mlb.edge_monotonicity()` Fisher test unmodified). **Result: the inversion is NOT concentrated in one game-state slice.** Both buckets reaching `MIN_N=30` verdict ANTI-PREDICTIVE independently: `inning=early_1-3` (n=136) and `ab_remaining=2+` (n=213, majority of cohort). `mid_4-6` (n=133) and `ab_remaining=1` (n=58) are directionally the same but INCONCLUSIVE at the Fisher bar; `late_7+` (n=2) UNDERPOWERED. Zero buckets verdicted MONOTONIC. Game-state correlations with edge/win were all weak (|r|≤0.13). **Two new data-quality gaps found and logged:** `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` has no score-differential field for T4M-PROJ either (mirrors the already-documented T4M-FLOW gap); `batting_slot` is hardcoded/near-constant `0` in 99.6% of all cycle-log rows, not a usable signal. GG-105 Resolution/Status updated in place. **Both candidate (a) game-state-noise and candidate (b) sizing-artifact are now weighed against** — the edge computation itself is the leading unexplained factor remaining. Full detail: DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-11 (T-332 row), GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md GG-105.
**Next recommended:** Diagnostic (3) — re-run as clean_n grows past its immature-cadence window (<14 days) — is the last of the three originally-scoped diagnostics and remains unstarted, but is a passive re-check rather than a build. Higher-value next step: a targeted look at T4M-PROJ's `p_t4`/edge/fee-net-edge formula itself (or an upstream feature feeding it), since both game-state and sizing have now been ruled against as external confounds and the anti-predictive rank-ordering has to originate somewhere.
**Depends on:** none.

## T-328 — Permanent deploy.yml fix for GG-104 (registered 2026-07-10)

**Do:** Add a post-`git pull` step to `deploy.yml` that unconditionally restarts both persistent systemd services on every successful deploy: `systemctl restart t4-mlb pmbot-web-dashboard`. Extend the existing "Verify HEAD" step to also assert `systemctl show <service> --property=ActiveEnterTimestamp` is more recent than the pull, so a future silent-staleness case is caught by the deploy pipeline itself rather than by manual `curl`/`journalctl` inspection.
**Why:** GG-104 (`03_DECISIONS/GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md`) — `deploy.yml` currently only confirms code-on-disk updated, not that long-running processes are executing it. This silently left the live T4M-PROJ evaluator running pre-fix `paper_trader.py` for ~2 days after the T-320/T-321 merge.
**Depends on:** none. Not urgent (manual restart is a working mitigation with a track record of safety), but should not be deferred indefinitely — every future write-path fix is at risk of the same silent-inertness failure mode until this lands.

**Prior — 2026-07-07 SESSION 124 — GG-092/093/094/095/096 triage — GG-095 RESOLVED (T-247), rest registered/deferred.** GG-095 fixed: operator directed "proceed with L4 design," and direct inspection of `monitor.py`'s own module docstring showed the 3-tier Brier ladder (L1 watch/L2 alert/L3 pause) was always the intended design — T-094's 2026-06-02 absolute/4-level entry is superseded, not `monitor.py`. The actual bug was narrow: `get_drift_snapshot()` assigned `ALERT_L4_KILL` (not `ALERT_L3_PAUSE`) at the `BRIER_L3_DELTA` crossing, so every L3-level Brier breach silently auto-fired a full kill switch instead of a pause-tier ERROR alert — `ALERT_L3_PAUSE` was fully wired in `check_and_alert()`'s logging maps but structurally unreachable. Fixed: L3 crossing now correctly sets `ALERT_L3_PAUSE`; the L4 auto-kill branch in `check_and_alert()` is retained (dormant, for a possible future integration) but can no longer fire from Brier drift alone. Confirmed via grep that the real L4/kill switch already exists and works correctly, independently, in `risk.py` (`MAX_DAILY_LOSS_PCT`/`max_drawdown_pct`) — matching `MONITORING_DRIFT_PLAN.md`'s ladder table exactly. 4 tests updated/added in `tests/test_c7_monitor.py`; full suite 2,374 passed/7 skipped/1 pre-existing unrelated failure. **Not built this session:** MONITORING_DRIFT_PLAN.md's L3 is meant to auto-halt new position opens specifically, not just log an event — no such enforcement (RiskEngine consulting C7's alert_level) exists in the codebase yet; this is a distinct C4↔C7 architectural decision flagged to the operator as an open follow-up, not built unilaterally. **GG-069 (OPEN, MEDIUM, no task number assigned yet) — `fetch_actual_hits`/`_fetch_mlb_hits` (`run_resolve_mlb.py`) has no FINAL-game-status gate.** It queries the MLB Stats API `gameLog` endpoint and returns whatever hit total is present for the matching `game_date`, with no check of `abstractGameState`/`codedGameState`. `gameLog` updates live mid-game, so in-progress games can and do resolve with partial hit totals. ## NEW TASKS — T4 MLB Throughput Diagnosis (set 2026-07-02, GG-076)

**Context:** Earl asked for a root-cause diagnosis of T4 MLB firing only ~10-20 bets/DAY against CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9's documented design of ~1,000 evaluations/game -> 50-100 fired bets/game. Diagnosed via direct analysis of real production data (`exp_output/MLB/snapshots/t4_mlb_cycle_log_SNAP-2026-07-02-002.jsonl`, 2,393 cycle-evaluations, 2026-07-02) plus source inspection of `run_t4_mlb_live.py`, `run_mlb_pregame.py` (PIPE-06pre), and `c4_risk_engine/risk.py`. Full finding registered as **GG-076**. Earl's ruling: CLAUDE.md's documented throughput target is authoritative; the implementation is what needs to change. "At optimum" per Earl = each bucket firing with correct math/filtering/decision trees/execution paths — not a P/L claim.

| T-225 | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-07-03.** Reconcile T4 MLB one-shot-per-ticker dedup (`already_fired_game` in `run_t4_mlb_live.py::_run_cycle`) against CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9 | DS (design decision) -> EL (implementation) | HIGH | **Earl's design ruling: "allow re-entry per ticker."** The permanent one-shot dedup was correct scaffolding while the Binomial math, contamination guards, kill switch timing, and C4 edge gate were unproven — it is not needed now that those are proven (kill switch drilled at 0.34ms, contamination guard verified, edge math confirmed responsive to real Kalshi price moves). The real backstops against over-concentration are `max_single_event_exposure_usd`, the drawdown kill switch, and the T4 band budget cap, not a blunt never-re-enter proxy. Implemented: `already_fired_game` changed from `set[str]` to `dict[str, tuple[int,int]]` mapping ticker → `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` at last entry; a ticker is blocked from re-firing only when the current cycle's `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` exactly matches its last-fired state, so re-entry is gated on the live state that actually feeds `p_t4` having moved. `PaperTrader.open_position()` gained optional `h_so_far_at_entry`/`ab_remaining_at_entry` kwargs (default `None`, backward-compatible with all non-T4 callers) so the guard survives a process restart; `_reconstruct_fired_tickers()` renamed `_reconstruct_fired_ticker_states()`, now scans `open_positions + closed_positions` sorted by `entered_at` so the most recent entry per ticker wins regardless of open/closed status. Verified no downstream code assumes ticker uniqueness: `run_resolve_mlb.py::_resolve_pool()` and `PaperTrader.settle_position()` both operate on individual `position_id`-keyed records, so multiple same-ticker positions resolve independently and correctly. Full rationale in DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-03 (3 entries: ruling, implementation design). 11 new tests added (`TestReconstructFiredTickerStates` x6, `TestRunCycle` re-entry x3, `TestOpenPosition` entry-state x2); full suite 2293 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (feature CSV staleness) — no regressions. See GG-076 (updated), T-229 (now unblocked, DS-owned). |

| T-230 | Fix silent skip-on-None in `_reconstruct_fired_ticker_states` for pre-T-225 positions | EL | **MEDIUM (status re-checked 2026-07-04 CONTINUATION — count/date-range corrected, exposure window now confirmed PERMANENTLY CLOSED for the historical positions found; fix remains deferred per Earl's standing "leave queued" choice)** | Found during independent post-deploy diff review (2026-07-03, confidence-gradient check, not caught by prior T-225/226/227 work): reconstruction filters out any position missing `h_so_far_at_entry`/`ab_remaining_at_entry` (i.e. any position opened before the T-225 deploy) entirely, rather than treating "state unknown" as a defensive re-fire block — `if ticker and h_so_far is not None and ab_remaining is not None: states[ticker] = (...)` silently drops the ticker instead of recording a conservative sentinel. Net effect: if a process restarts mid-game while an open position exists from before this field existed, `already_fired_game.get(ticker)` returns `None` (not found), and `None == (ls.h_so_far, ab_remaining)` is never True, so the guard does not block a same-cycle re-fire on a ticker that may already hold an open position. **Correction (2026-07-03, T-231 session): the "no known live exposure" claim above was wrong** — found 10 positions, all `game_date=2026-07-03`. **Second correction (2026-07-04 CONTINUATION, item-2 status re-check): the T-231 session's "10 positions, 07-03-only" figure was itself an undercount.** Direct query against the live `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` on the VPS (all `open_positions` + `closed_positions` with `h_so_far_at_entry=None` or `ab_remaining_at_entry=None`) found **35 positions**, spanning **three game dates — 2026-07-01, 2026-07-02, and 2026-07-03** — not one. The T-231 session's check evidently only looked at the most recent date. Cross-checked all 11 open positions dated 2026-07-04 (the current live date at time of this re-check, confirmed via `date -u` and `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl`'s most recent entries): all 11 have clean, populated `h_so_far_at_entry`/`ab_remaining_at_entry` — the T-225 fix is confirmed working correctly for every position opened since its real restart. **Why this is nonetheless still safe to leave queued, not urgent:** Kalshi KXMLBHIT tickers encode the specific game date in the string itself (e.g. `KXMLBHIT-26JUL02...`), so a ticker from a 07-01/07-02/07-03 game can never be polled or re-evaluated by any live cycle again — those game-dates are permanently in the past relative to any future T4 cycle. This means the 35-position gap is not merely "dormant today," it is **structurally unable to ever produce a live duplicate-re-entry event**, for these specific 35 positions. The underlying code defect (silent None-skip instead of a blocking sentinel) remains real and would recur for any *future* schema migration to this dict — that risk is what stays open, not these 35 positions specifically. **Side finding (new, out of T-230's original scope, surfaced while re-verifying it — registered separately, see GG-079/T-234, not fixed here):** 3 of the 07-01 positions in this same gap carry `kalshi_order_status: "failed: HTTP 410"` (order never executed — market gone by submission time) yet remain counted in `open_positions` rather than a rejected/failed bucket. Fix for the original T-230 defect unchanged from prior sessions: on missing fields, insert a sentinel that can never equal a real `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` pair (e.g. `(-1, -1)`) so an unknown-state ticker defaults to blocked, not open. Still deferred per Earl's explicit "leave queued" choice — this re-check confirms that choice remains safe (zero live exposure now or forecastably), it does not change the recommendation to fix opportunistically rather than urgently.

**GG-067 registered and resolved (doc correction only, no code implication):** two false data-source claims discovered in the T4M-ARITH/T4M-FLOW experiment design docs — (1) both docs claimed a per-cycle raw context log already existed; it did not — only `dry_run=True` persisted anything, and that path is not the live production path. (2) `EXP-FLOW-MLB-01.md` additionally claimed `t4_price_cache.py` was the live MLB Kalshi price source; it is not — that module is Phase-8-BLOCKED NBA-oriented infra, never imported by `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s live loop, which instead populates `live_prices: dict` directly from `pm.mid_price`/`pm.vig`. Both docs corrected to cite the real source, `logs/t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl`, once built (see next).

**Production fix (T-219 continuation):** added unconditional per-cycle raw-context logging to `run_t4_mlb_live.py` — every live evaluation cycle now appends a JSONL record (including a `game_date` field, added mid-session after a schema-gap review) to `logs/t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl`. New tests added (`TestProductionCycleLog`, including `_REQUIRED_FIELDS` schema assertion). This is what makes the GG-067 doc corrections true rather than aspirational — the log now actually exists in the live code path.

**Backtest runner built:** `scripts/run_exp_arith_mlb_01.py` (36 tests, 36/36 passing) grid-sweeps T4M-ARITH's `entry_window`/`confidence_gate` constants against `logs/t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl`, resolving real outcomes via `fetch_actual_hits` (MLB Stats API) through a dependency-injected resolver (`build_resolution_cache(cycles, resolver)`) so CI makes zero live network calls. Key design insight: because `T4MlbArithEvaluator` reuses the exact same frozen binomial `p_hits_gte_threshold(...)` function with the exact same inputs as the already-live T4M-PROJ evaluation, a cycle-log record's `p_t4` field is mathematically identical to `p_arith` for that record — no separate arithmetic re-derivation needed in the backtest. Applies ADR-0024 commercial validation tiers (Tier 1 n≥150, Tier 2 n≥500) and the T-177/GG-037 edge-monotonicity guard (Fisher's exact test → MONOTONIC/ANTI-PREDICTIVE/INCONCLUSIVE) to whatever data accumulates. Chronological (no-shuffle) splitting per project-wide temporal-integrity policy. Kalshi taker fee (`0.07 × mip × (1-mip)`) applied in P&L.

**Result as of this session's close: BLOCKED, not run — by design, not by omission.** The cycle log had zero records at deploy time because the logging feature only went live with this session's own deploy, and no MLB game was in progress at the 16:34 UTC deploy moment. `T4M-ARITH` correctly remains `status="BLOCKED"` in the ADR-0039 sub-band table — infra/code authorization is not the same thing as data existence, which is not the same thing as capital authorization. **Continuation of the T-217 thread in a fresh context window, per operator directive: "T-211 is high priority but requires a new task number and its own ADR... scope MLB-native T4 sub-bands before writing code." VPS deploy of the T-217 fix completed first (see below), then T-218 scoping.**

Confirmed safe restart window first (all games `STATUS_SCHEDULED` at restart time).
- **T-218 registered, ADR-0039 drafted** (`03_DECISIONS/ADR-0039_mlb-native-t4-subband-architecture-scoping.md`). Read `run_t4_mlb_live.py` directly first (applying GG-061's process fix prospectively — verify target code's actual domain before authorizing changes) — confirmed it's a single flat Binomial evaluator (`KELLY_T4=0.10`, one 45s ESPN-poll loop), the same "monolithic T4" problem ADR-0029 fixed for NBA, never inherited by MLB because MLB T4 was built independently (T-190/T-203/T-204).
- ADR-0039 does **not** copy the NBA roster verbatim. Four structural differences drove an MLB-native taxonomy: (1) MLB C1 is poll-only (45s ESPN diff), not event-push; (2) MLB's core signal (a resolved at-bat) is a deterministic state transition, not a probabilistic nudge like an NBA field goal; (3) pitcher identity/platoon split is an MLB-unique info class; (4) PA frequency is slot-gated/sparse (~3.1-4.2/game via `_AB_BY_SLOT`), organized around innings not a continuous clock.
- 10-sub-band Tier B roster proposed (T4M-PROJ reclassifying the existing flat model as the architectural spine, plus EVENT/PITCHER/LINEUP/FLOW/BOUNDARY/ARITH/STAGED/CORR/EXIT), all EXP-required per Principle 5 — no numerics proposed. Tier A (three-layer architecture, Principles 1-6) adopted unchanged from ADR-0029 as sport-agnostic; ≤5s kill-switch SLA explicitly flagged as NOT inherited (was derived from NBA's continuous per-second event density) — provisionally retains the existing file-based `t4_mlb_kill_switch.flag` (halts within one 45s cycle) pending re-derivation.
- **GG-066 registered** (OPEN): MLB C1 has no real-time/sub-poll-interval event feed — blocks T4M-EVENT, T4M-LINEUP, and real-time T4M-ARITH firing. Candidate resolution: MLB Stats API live game feed v1.1. T4M-BOUNDARY and T4M-ARITH flagged as cheapest next build (poll-driven, no new data source required). Also flagged but not registered as its own gap: `_AB_BY_SLOT` is 9-inning-only and understates `ab_remaining` in extra innings — low-effort fix candidate independent of this ADR.
- **No code authorized.** T-218 is scoping-only; each sub-band requires its own data-prerequisite resolution, EXP, and Phase 8 Activation Gate before implementation.
- DECISION_LOG.md SESSION 108/T-218 entry written (hard rule #7). **ADR-0039's own Status line reads "ACCEPTED (scoping only)" but its DS Sign-Off section says "Pending — to be confirmed at session close" — this is an unresolved internal inconsistency. Must be explicitly confirmed with Earl next session, not silently treated as accepted.**

- Fix: `kalshi_client.py submit_order()` migrated to `POST /portfolio/events/orders` (v2) with correct schema (`bid`/`ask` side, fixed-point decimal `count`/`price` strings, `time_in_force`, `self_trade_prevention_type`), plus the originally-scoped error-body threading (`_post()`'s `body`/`detail` now reaches the caller instead of being discarded behind a bare `f"HTTP {r.status_code}"`).
- Verified empirically against DEMO with a real manual test order: error type changed 410→409 (`market_closed`, all 60 open KXMLBHIT markets were next-day at test time) — confirms the v1 deprecation wall is gone; full 200/201 confirmation deferred to the next live cron run against a genuinely active market. Ticker-mismatch hypothesis explicitly disproven (`GET /markets/{ticker}` returned HTTP 200 on both DEMO and LIVE).

Re-evaluation run on full 3-season feature matrix (`exp_output/MLB/mlb_feature_matrix.csv`, 126,421 rows, holdout cutoff 2026-01-01). Shadow gate outcome: t=3 Brier delta=+0.0002 (FAIL — same as T-187B), ECE=0.0037 (PASS), AUC=0.6141. Root cause is structural: at 3.9% base rate, even AUC=0.61 cannot produce absolute Brier delta ≥0.001 — this is an information-theoretic ceiling, not a data quality problem. **No code changes.** `THRESHOLDS = [1, 2]` and `_EXCLUDED_THRESHOLDS = [3]` in `train_mlb_model.py` unchanged; `_MODEL_THRESHOLDS = frozenset({1, 2})` in `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` unchanged. (5) This is a feature engineering gap, NOT a model class failure. Poisson architecture is valid; the λ input is wrong. |

| Curses ordering bug fix | **Fixed blank-panel rendering: stdscr erased content areas; pad writes were invisible** | ✅ COMPLETE 2026-06-03 SESSION 27 — Root cause: `draw_panel()` called `ps.pad.noutrefresh()` inside each panel draw, then `stdscr.noutrefresh()` ran last → stdscr's blank content areas overwrote pad content in curses' virtual screen buffer → `doupdate()` rendered blank panels despite data present. Fix: moved all pad `noutrefresh` calls to a separate `refresh_pad()` pass that runs AFTER `stdscr.noutrefresh()`. stdscr wins on borders; pads win on content. Data confirmed flowing: 1416 T4 positions, $499.95 deployed, 3 T1 bets. |

## SESSION 2026-06-02 SESSION 13 — OPEN TASKS (dark period / Phase 8 pre-activation)

| # | Task | Owner | Priority | Notes |
|---|------|-------|----------|-------|
| T-128 | **DS-02 ADR: require live rolling pace as mandatory feed field** | DS | **HIGH — Phase 8 pre-activation** | KI-024: `pace_pts_per_min` is set at T1 from C2 feature store and never refreshed during a game. λ = pace × time_remaining drives both T4 and T5 Poisson CDF — a stale pace propagates linearly into every in-game probability estimate. At T5 (≤3 min remaining), λ is small and pace error translates directly to P(hit threshold) error. The Phase 8 DS-02 ADR must explicitly require live rolling pace as a required feed field, not optional enrichment. Without this constraint, a DS-02 source that provides only score + clock (no pace) would silently degrade T4/T5 inference. Resolution: operator reviews DS-02 ADR draft and adds live pace as a required field before signing. |
| EXP-018 | **T4_MIN_DELTA_P calibration experiment** | DS | **MEDIUM — Phase 8 pre-activation** | GG-007 (item 1): T4_MIN_DELTA_P=0.08 retained for paper prototype to generate calibration data. Requires replay of historical NBA game-state snapshots. Gate: must complete before Phase 8 T4 activation ADR is signed. |
| EXP-019 | **T5 certainty threshold calibration experiment** | DS | **MEDIUM — Phase 8 pre-activation** | GG-007 (item 2): T5_YES_CERTAINTY=0.85 / T5_NO_CERTAINTY=0.15 grounded by Poisson arithmetic (SESSION 14). Requires calibration against historical Q4 ≤3 min game-state data. Gate: must complete before Phase 8 T5 activation ADR is signed. |

## Standing Items (Monthly — GG-074 process fix, adopted 2026-07-06)

- [ ] **Grounding-docs staleness review.** Re-run a file-by-file staleness audit across `01_SPEC/`, `02_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN/`, `07_TEST_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE/`, `08_MODEL_RISK_GOVERNANCE/` — the structured, point-in-time documents that describe the system's designed shape, as distinct from the rolling logs (MEMORY_BANK.md, this file, GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md, DECISION_LOG.md) that already get updated every session. Tie this to the same cadence as MEMORY_BANK.md's Compass OS Health M1 reporting. Rationale: GG-074 found 19+ files had silently drifted from reality over 5+ weeks with no session-start check catching it, because each document's own "staleness trigger" (e.g., "gate passed," "ADR accepted") fired without prompting a re-read of the document that named the trigger. The rolling-log discipline (Hard Rule 7, session-start Step 1) does not cover this failure mode — it needs its own standing trigger, independent of any single gate closing.
